```
# Anelasticity restart 2026-10-05 (written 17:20 PDT)

Read with `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ANELASTICITY_TODO.md`, `ANELASTICITY_DONE.md` and the physics/plan document `ANELASTICITY_PLAN.md` (equations, noise model, sweep design). All in `/resnick/home/recohen/Claude/Anelasticity`.

## 0. What to check FIRST (in order)

1. **Speed of DeePMD on the P100 is unresolved (T1).** The timing test (job 4014215, cancelled after 100 steps) ran `~/bin/lmp` with the conda env `deepmd_py311` and took **3.86 s/step** for 4096 atoms with the uncompressed `graph.pb`. Old 4096-atom logs run at 7–19 steps/s (0.05–0.14 s/step), about 30–75× faster.
   - Facts: LAMMPS 22Jul2025 build has `PKG_GPU=yes, GPU_API=cuda, GPU_ARCH=sm_60` (P100). The deepmd-kit summary in the run log says `build variant: cpu`; `libdeepmd_cc.so`, `libdeepmd_op.so`, `libdeepmd_c.so` in the env contain no CUDA symbols; `libtensorflow_cc.so.2` does. No TensorFlow "Created device ... GPU" line appeared in the log.
   - RC (17:15): "It is gpu build and so is lmp!!" — the LAMMPS part is confirmed GPU. Whether DeePMD's network actually ran on the GPU in that job is **not established**. The LAMMPS GPU package does not accelerate `pair_style deepmd`.
   - The job script set `PATH` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` to the conda env only. It did **not** `module load cuda` and did not set the CUDNN path that the old scripts set. This is the first suspect.
   - Next step: a 15-minute P100 diagnostic with `TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL=0`, `module load cuda`, and `nvidia-smi` sampling during the run (T1).
2. The old GPU deepmd-kit still exists: `/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/deepmd-kit/bin/lmp` (Aug 2025, has `libdeepmd_op_cuda.so`). It has no `dp` binary.
3. `squeue -u recohen`: no anelasticity jobs should be running. Nothing from the sweep has been submitted.
```

### Claude (10-06 17:28)

I found why LAMMPS isn't using the GPU. You don't need to move this session: I ran a short test on your GPU node (job 4099863, hpc-21-19) from here with `srun --overlap`.

**What's wrong.** The env `deepmd_py311` holds two TensorFlow 2.21.0 builds:
- **pip, GPU** in `site-packages/tensorflow`. It's CUDA 12, uses abseil `lts_20250814` built into it, and the library contains P100 (`sm_60`) code.
- **conda-forge, CPU** (`cpu_` packages) as `libtensorflow_cc.so.2` and `libtensorflow_framework.so.2` in `$CONDA_PREFIX/lib`. It uses conda's separate abseil `lts_20260107`.

Your deepmd 3.2.0 build reports `build variant: cuda`, but it is tied to the **CPU** TensorFlow in two ways:
1. **Library search path.** The deepmd libraries search `$CONDA_PREFIX/lib` before `site-packages/tensorflow`, so they load the conda CPU TensorFlow. This search path also overrides `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, so changing the job script can't fix it. TensorFlow never creates a GPU device. The 254 MiB on the GPU is only the CUDA runtime starting up, with 0% use.
2. **Headers.** The build compiled against conda's abseil headers (`-I$CONDA_PREFIX/include` and `TENSORFLOW_ROOT=$CONDA_PREFIX`). When I forced the pip GPU libraries in with `LD_PRELOAD`, deepmd's TensorFlow plugin failed to load: `libdeepmd_backend_tf.so: undefined symbol absl::lts_20260107::...StatusRep::Unref`.

**Fix: rebuild deepmd C++ against the pip TensorFlow only.** The pip package has its own `absl`, `Eigen` and `google/protobuf` headers, so nothing should come from conda's include directory.
1. Use a new build directory, e.g. `source/build_gpu`, with:
   - `-DENABLE_TENSORFLOW=ON -DUSE_TF_PYTHON_LIBS=TRUE -DTENSORFLOW_ROOT=$CONDA_PREFIX/lib/python3.11/site-packages/tensorflow`
   - `-DUSE_CUDA_TOOLKIT=TRUE -DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=60 -DLAMMPS_SOURCE_ROOT=…22Jul2025_update2`
   - Remove `-I$CONDA_PREFIX/include` and `-I$CONDA_PREFIX/include/eigen3` from `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS`.
   - Check that the `Findtensorflow.cmake` edits you made in git don't point back at conda's headers.
2. Move the conda CPU `libtensorflow*.so*` from `$CONDA_PREFIX/lib` into a backup folder. This is reversible. The search path then falls through to the pip GPU libraries.
3. `make install`, then rerun the 200-step test on your GPU node. Pass means `Created device … Tesla P100` in the log and an `lmp` process using GPU memory and compute.

The test files are in `/resnick/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/Downloads/gputest`; `run.sh` is the script. I haven't changed your environment or source tree.

Your other session is building in the same tree, so I stopped there. Should I do steps 1–3, or will you run them over there? The conda `tensorflow` packages are installed in the same `site-packages` as the pip one. **Don't `conda remove` them:** that would delete files the pip install overwrote.

Also, `--exclude=hpc-93-36` is commented out in `1submit_lammps.sh`, and job 4103459 ran on that node.

## User (10-06 17:32)

**Tool `Bash`** — Configure deepmd GPU build against pip TF

```
B=/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/deepmd-kit/source/build_gpu; mkdir -p $B; cat > $B/configure_gpu.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# deepmd-kit v3.2.0 C++/LAMMPS build against the pip CUDA TensorFlow in deepmd_py311 (P100, sm_60).
# Conda compiler flags are stripped so conda's CPU TensorFlow/abseil headers are not used.
source /resnick/home/recohen/.bashrc >/dev/null 2>&1
shopt -s expand_aliases
module load cuda
conda activate deepmd_py311
TF=$CONDA_PREFIX/lib/python3.11/site-packages/tensorflow
export CFLAGS="-O2 -fPIC" CXXFLAGS="-O2 -fPIC" CPPFLAGS="" CUDAFLAGS=""
export LDFLAGS="-Wl,-rpath,$TF -Wl,--disable-new-dtags"
cd $(dirname $0)
cmake -DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=$CONDA_PREFIX \
  -DENABLE_TENSORFLOW=ON -DUSE_TF_PYTHON_LIBS=TRUE -DTENSORFLOW_ROOT=$TF \
  -DUSE_CUDA_TOOLKIT=TRUE -DCUDAToolkit_ROOT=$CUDA_HOME -DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=60 \
  -DLAMMPS_SOURCE_ROOT=/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/lammps-stable_22Jul2025_update2 \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release ..
EOF
chmod +x $B/configure_gpu.sh; $B/configure_gpu.sh > $B/configure.log 2>&1; echo rc=$?; grep -iE "tensorflow|protobuf|abi|cuda|error|warn" $B/configure.log | head -40
```

### Claude (10-06 17:45)

LAMMPS with DeePMD now runs on the P100. My 200-step 4096-atom test on your GPU node reached **8.1 steps/s (0.12 s/step)**, versus 3.73 s/step before, about 30× faster. That's within the 7–19 steps/s of your old runs. TensorFlow logged `Created device … Tesla P100 … compute capability: 6.0`, and `nvidia-smi` showed `lmp` using 4.4 GB at 70–98% GPU. I gave it only 4 CPU threads, so the 9-thread production script may be a bit faster.

**Why it wasn't working:** the env had two TensorFlow 2.21 builds, and the conda CPU one always won.
- **Libraries:** the conda CPU libraries sat in `deepmd_py311/lib`. The env's activate script puts that directory first in `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, so LAMMPS loaded the CPU libraries, not your pip GPU TensorFlow. Python's `import tensorflow` failed in the activated env for the same reason.
- **Build:** your deepmd 3.2.0 build had compiled against conda's headers, so it was also tied to the CPU build. Your `Findtensorflow.cmake` edit and the conda compiler's `-isystem $CONDA_PREFIX/include` brought them in.

**What I changed:**
1. **Moved the conda CPU TensorFlow libraries aside** (`libtensorflow*.so*`) into `/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/anaconda2/deepmd_py311_condaCPUtf_backup_2026-10-06/lib`. Moving them back undoes it.
2. **Rebuilt deepmd-kit v3.2.0** from your tree, `deepmd-kit/source/build_gpu`. It builds against the pip TensorFlow only, with CUDA for the P100 (`sm_60`), and installs into `deepmd_py311` as before. The scripts are `configure_gpu.sh` and `make_gpu.sh`. Your `Findtensorflow.cmake` edits are left as they were; this build doesn't reach them.
3. **Backed up the previous deepmd install** at `…backup_2026-10-06/prev_install/prev_deepmd_install.tgz`.

**Things to watch:**
- `source/build`, configured with `TENSORFLOW_ROOT=$CONDA_PREFIX`, would no longer build. Use `build_gpu` from now on.
- I didn't rebuild the PyTorch backend libraries (`libdeepmd_backend_pt*.so`). Your `graph.pb` is a TensorFlow model and doesn't use them.
- Your `1submit_lammps.sh` should work unchanged. Its `CUDNN_PATH` / `/home/recohen/lib` `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` line is no longer needed, but it does no harm. `--exclude=hpc-93-36` is still commented out.

Test files are in `/resnick/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/Downloads/gputest`. With a measured speed, I can now re-derive the frequency-sweep cost (T1 in the restart notes).

```
3:Started 2026-10-05. System: MgO, 4096 atoms, $V = 6.6868$ Å$^3$/atom (constant volume, about 190 GPa at 8000 K), DeePMD potential `graph.pb` (Mg, O; $r_c = 7$ Å), LAMMPS `~/bin/lmp` (22Jul2025) with conda env `deepmd_py311`, Tesla P100.
19:with relaxation function $G(t) = G_e + \int H(\tau)e^{-t/\tau}\,d\ln\tau$ and $G^*(\omega) = i\omega\int_0^\infty G(t)e^{-i\omega t}dt$. Green–Kubo gives $G(t) = (V/k_BT)\langle\sigma_{xz}(0)\sigma_{xz}(t)\rangle$ directly, so it is the equilibrium cross-check of Eq. (4).
58:## 4. Frequency sweep
60:`~/Claude/tools/anelastic_sweep_setup.py` writes task directories, `tasks.txt` and a SLURM array `sweep.sh` (user's P100 header). `anelastic_sweep.lammps` equilibrates 20 ps NVT, then runs xz wiggle + `nvt/sllod` in whole-period chunks with a restart after each chunk. Default sweep at 8000 K: periods 0.2, 1, 5, 20, 100 ps (5 THz to 10 GHz, 2.7 decades), 10% target error on $G''$, at least 3 cycles and 20 ps per point. Cost at the old uncompressed P100 speed (0.115 s/step) is about 0.6 M steps, 0.8 P100-days in total, under a day of wall time as an array. A linearity check repeats the 20 ps point at $\dot\gamma_0 = 3\times10^{10}$ s$^{-1}$.
62:The cost floor is set by cycles, not noise: one period at frequency $f$ costs $1/(f\,\Delta t)$ steps, i.e. $10^6$ steps (about 1.3 P100-days) per period at 1 GHz. Frequencies below 10 GHz are therefore only run where they carry information, i.e. at lower $T$ where $\tau$ has grown.
66:- Timing of the compressed model (`dp compress`) on P100 sets the real cost.
3:- **T1 (blocking): establish DeePMD speed on the P100.** First run (3.86 s/step) used a hand-built environment and is void. Rerun as job 4021504 from RC's R1A1 script with only the env swapped to `conda activate deepmd_py311` (dir `8000K/FreqSweep/timing/p100_env`), timing `graph.pb` and `graph_c.pb`.
5:- **T3: recompute sweep cost** with the measured speed. Present it to RC before submitting.
6:- **T4: 8000 K sweep** (after RC approval): periods 0.2, 1, 5, 20, 100 ps, plus a linearity repeat at 20 ps with $\dot\gamma_0=3\times10^{10}$ s$^{-1}$. Generator: `anelastic_sweep_setup.py`.
7:- **T5: first MgO test of `anelastic_sweep.lammps`** (short, one period) to confirm restart, `append` files and the lock-in on real output.
```

### Claude (10-06 18:06)

At 8.1 steps/s, the 8000 K sweep costs about **1.6 P100-days in total**. Run as an array, it finishes in about **17 hours of wall time**.

The numbers come from `anelastic_sweep_setup.py` with 0.1235 s/step, and each task includes its 20k-step equilibration:

| Period (ps) | Frequency | Strain amplitude γ₀ | Run (ps) | Cycles | Steps | Wall time (h) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2 | 5 THz | 0.010 | 20 | 100 | 40 k | 1.4 |
| 1 | 1 THz | 0.016 | 60 | 60 | 80 k | 2.7 |
| 5 | 200 GHz | 0.080 | 60 | 12 | 80 k | 2.7 |
| 20 | 50 GHz | 0.32 | 60 | 3 | 80 k | 2.7 |
| 100 | 10 GHz | 1.59 | 300 | 3 | 320 k | 11.0 |
| 20, linearity check (γ̇₀ = 3×10¹⁰ s⁻¹) | 50 GHz | 0.095 | 480 | 24 | 500 k | 17.1 |

- **Total:** 1.1 M steps, which is 37.7 P100-hours.
- **The linearity check costs as much as all five sweep points together.** The lower strain rate gives a smaller signal, so it needs 8× the run time to reach 10% error on G″. Without it the total is 0.6 M steps (0.9 P100-days), and the 100 ps point sets the wall time at about 11 h. We could run it more cheaply at a looser error, or only after the main sweep.
- **The 100 ps point shears far:** a strain amplitude of 1.6 tilts the box by 48 Å on a 30 Å box. That depends on the box flips I checked on the LJ liquid but haven't yet run on MgO.
- **The speed may be slightly pessimistic.** It was measured with 4 CPU threads and the uncompressed `graph.pb`. The production script uses 9 threads, and the compressed model may be faster still.

Nothing has been submitted. The table is now in section 0f of the restart notes, and the dry-run task directories are in `Downloads/sweep_cost_8p1` and `Downloads/sweep_cost_8p1_lin`. Should I submit all six tasks, the five sweep points without the linearity check, or first a short one-period test on MgO (T5) to check restarts and the lock-in on real output?

## User (10-06 18:10)

Run without the linearity test first.

### Claude (10-06 18:36)

All five tasks of array 4109686 are running at **8.26 steps/s (0.121 s/step)**, slightly faster than the 8.1 test. The rate is the same on every node.

- **How I got it:** LAMMPS's remaining-time estimate falls by about 121 s per 1000 steps. The tasks are about 11,000 steps into the 20,000-step equilibration after roughly 22 minutes, which agrees.
- **GPU:** TensorFlow logged `Created device … Tesla P100` in all five, and each `lmp` uses 4.4 GB on its GPU.
- **Expected run times:**
  - 0.2 ps point: about 1.3 h
  - 1, 5 and 20 ps points: about 2.7 h each
  - 100 ps point: about 10.8 h
- **Not measured yet:** this speed is from the equilibration stage. The oscillating-shear stage runs extra fixes (`nvt/sllod`, `fix deform`), so it may be a little slower. It starts at step 20,000, about 20 minutes from now.
- **Where to watch progress:** use `slurm-4109686_<i>.out`. The `log.lammps.*` files are written in large blocks and still stop at step 1000.

I changed `anelastic_sweep_setup.py` so the job script it writes matches your working GPU script:
- it runs `$CONDA_PREFIX/bin/lmp` and sets `LAMMPS_PLUGIN_PATH`;
- it drops `load_intel`;
- it mails ALL;
- it records `nvidia-smi` every 5 minutes.

The submission and speed are in section 0g of the restart notes. Once the oscillation stage starts I'll check its speed and the first box flips in the 20 and 100 ps tasks, then run the lock-in analysis on the 0.2 ps point when it finishes.

```
  -i INPUT, --input INPUT
                        The original frozen model or checkpoint, which will be compressed by the code. Filename (prefix) of the input model file. TensorFlow backend: suffix is .pb; TensorFlow2 backend: .tf2 checkpoint directory or checkpoint prefix; PyTorch backend: suffix is .pth; DPModel backend: suffix is .dp; JAX backend: suffix is .hlo or .jax (default: frozen_model)
  -o OUTPUT, --output OUTPUT
                        The compressed model. Filename (prefix) of the output model file. TensorFlow backend: suffix is .pb; TensorFlow2 backend: suffix is .savedmodeltf; PyTorch backend: suffix is .pth; DPModel backend: suffix is .dp; JAX backend: suffix is .hlo or .jax (default: frozen_model_compressed)
  -s STEP, --step STEP  Model compression uses fifth-order polynomials to interpolate the embedding-net. It introduces two tables with different step size to store the parameters of the polynomials. The first table covers the range of the training data, while the second table is an extrapolation of the training data. The domain of each table is uniformly divided by a given step size. And the step(parameter) denotes the step size of the first table and the second table will use 10 * step as it's step size to save the memory. Usually the value ranges from 0.1 to 0.001. Smaller step means higher accuracy and bigger model size (default: 0.01)
  -e EXTRAPOLATE, --extrapolate EXTRAPOLATE
                        The domain range of the first table is automatically detected by the code: [d_low, d_up]. While the second table ranges from the first table's upper boundary(d_up) to the extrapolate(parameter) * d_up: [d_up, extrapolate * d_up] (default: 5)
  -f FREQUENCY, --frequency FREQUENCY
                        The frequency of tabulation overflow check(Whether the input environment matrix overflow the first or second table range). By default do not check the overflow (default: -1)
--
  -t TRAINING_SCRIPT, --training-script TRAINING_SCRIPT
                        The training script of the input frozen model (default: None)
```

### Claude (10-06 18:43)

**Directory:** it's the same directory under both names. `/groups` is a symlink to `/resnick/groups/`, so the sweep is running in
`/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/LAMMPS/MgO/4096atoms/V6.6868/4096atom/8000K/FreqSweep`
with task subdirectories `P0.2ps_r0`, `P1ps_r0`, `P5ps_r0`, `P20ps_r0` and `P100ps_r0`. The `/resnick/groups/...` form came from `$SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR`. I've switched the restart notes and my saved notes to the `/groups/...` form and will use it from now on.

**Sweep status:** array 4112680 is running. TensorFlow found the P100 in all 5 tasks, and they are at about 21–22 steps/s with 9 threads. That's about the same as the 4-thread test, so the CPU thread count barely matters here.

**Making a compressed potential:** do it once per model, in the `deepmd_py311` env. It took 26 s here.
```bash
conda activate deepmd_py311
dp --tf compress -i graph.pb -o graph_c.pb
```
Then use `pair_style deepmd graph_c.pb` in the LAMMPS input.

- **`--tf`** selects the TensorFlow backend, which is what a `.pb` model uses.
- **`-s` (step, default 0.01)** is the table spacing; smaller is more accurate and bigger. The default reproduced your model to 0.0003 eV in total energy at step 0 for 4096 atoms.
- **`-e` (extrapolate, default 5)** sets how far the table extends beyond the training data's range.
- **`-f N`** checks every N steps whether the run leaves the table's range. It's off by default; `-f 1000` is cheap insurance at extreme conditions.
- **`-t input.json`** passes the training script. It's needed only if the frozen model doesn't contain it, and this one did.
- **Check each new compressed model** by comparing step-0 energy and pressure with the uncompressed one, as we did here.

## User (10-06 18:47)

run the lock-in analysis when P0.2 finishes. Are you doing the xy or x strain?

### Claude (10-06 18:47)

The sweep applies **xz shear**: `fix deform 1 xz wiggle`, so the tilt factor xz oscillates. It's neither the xy shear nor the uniaxial x strain of your R1A1/R0.1A1 runs. It measures the shear modulus G\* from σ_xz against γ = xz/L_z. Your 54671682 run used the same kind of strain. Uniaxial x gives the longitudinal M = K + (4/3)G instead. xz and xy are equivalent by symmetry in a liquid.

Checking how the lock-in tool reads the sweep's output, then waiting for P0.2 to finish:

**Tool `Bash`** — Check lock-in tool usage

```
T=/resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools/anelastic_lockin.py; sed -n 1,40p $T | grep -vE "^import|^$"; grep -nE "add_argument|stress.dat|strain.dat" $T | head -30
```

<details><summary>result of Bash</summary>

```
This line only runs in interactive mode.
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Lock-in analysis of oscillatory-deformation MD (LAMMPS fix deform wiggle).
Fits stress and strain to  a + b t + sum_k [s_k sin(k w t) + c_k cos(k w t)]
at the KNOWN drive angular frequency w = 2 pi / period, k = 1..nharm.
Complex amplitudes at k=1 give the complex modulus
    M* = sigma_1 / eps_1 = M' + i M''      (Eq. 1)
    tan(delta) = M'' / M'                  (Eq. 2)
    eta'(w)    = M'' / w                   (Eq. 3)
Shear (xz): M* = G*.  Uniaxial (x): M* = K* + 4/3 G*  and M''/w = zeta + 4/3 eta.
Stress sign: sigma = -P_ab (LAMMPS pressure tensor), bar -> Pa (x1e5).
Error bars: the series is cut into nblock blocks of whole cycles and each block
is fit independently; std/sqrt(nblock) is reported.
Nonlinearity: |sigma_2|/|sigma_1| and |sigma_3|/|sigma_1| are printed.
Input: whitespace columns with a header line naming columns, e.g. the output of
extract_thermo.sh (Step ... Pxz ... Xz) or a fix ave/time file
(# TimeStep v_pxz v_xz v_lz).  Column names are matched case-insensitively
after stripping 'v_', 'c_', 'f_' prefixes.
Usage:
  anelastic_lockin.py FILE --period PS [--mode shear|uniaxial] [--dt 0.001] [--cols a,b,..]
                      [--L0 Å] [--skip-cycles 2] [--nblock 5] [--nharm 3]
"""
def read_table(fname, cols=None):
    header = None
    rows = []
    with open(fname) as f:
        for line in f:
            s = line.strip()
            if not s:
                continue
            toks = s.lstrip('#').split()
74:    """stress.dat (bin averages) + strain.dat (instantaneous) from anelastic_sweep.lammps."""
75:    ns, S = read_aveTime(f'{d}/stress.dat')
76:    ne, E = read_aveTime(f'{d}/strain.dat')
129:    p.add_argument('file', help='thermo table, or a run directory with stress.dat+strain.dat '
```

```
This line only runs in interactive mode.
    return names, np.array([rows[k] for k in steps])


def load_sweepdir(d):
    """stress.dat (bin averages) + strain.dat (instantaneous) from anelastic_sweep.lammps."""
    ns, S = read_aveTime(f'{d}/stress.dat')
    ne, E = read_aveTime(f'{d}/strain.dat')
    common = np.intersect1d(S[:, 0], E[:, 0])
    S = S[np.isin(S[:, 0], common)]
    E = E[np.isin(E[:, 0], common)]
    xz, lx, lz = (E[:, ne.index(k)] for k in ('xz', 'lx', 'lz'))
    # unwrap box flips: xz jumps by -+lx
    jump = np.diff(xz)
    corr = np.concatenate([[0.0], np.cumsum(-np.round(jump / lx[1:]) * lx[1:])])
    xz_un = xz + corr
    return S[:, 0], xz_un / lz, S[:, ns.index('pxz')]


def col(names, data, *cands):
    for c in cands:
        if c in names:
            return data[:, names.index(c)]
    return None


def design(t, w, nharm):
    cols = [np.ones_like(t), t - t.mean()]
    for k in range(1, nharm + 1):
        cols += [np.sin(k * w * t), np.cos(k * w * t)]
    return np.vstack(cols).T


def fit(t, y, w, nharm):
    A = design(t, w, nharm)
    c, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(A, y, rcond=None)
    amps = c[2::2] + 1j * c[3::2]          # complex amplitude, sin reference
    resid = y - A @ c
    return amps, resid.std()


def boxcar(w, nav, dt):
    """complex response of an NAV-step trailing average sampled at its last step."""
    if nav <= 1:
        return 1.0
    x = w * dt
    return np.sin(nav * x / 2) / (nav * np.sin(x / 2)) * np.exp(-1j * x * (nav - 1) / 2)


def analyse(t, eps, sig, w, nharm, nav=1, dt=0.001):
    ea, _ = fit(t, eps, w, nharm)
    sa, noise = fit(t, sig, w, nharm)
    sa = sa / np.array([boxcar(k * w, nav, dt) for k in range(1, nharm + 1)])
    M = sa[0] / ea[0]
    return M, ea, sa, noise


def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,
                                formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
```

```
This line only runs in interactive mode.
#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Set up a frequency sweep of oscillatory-shear NEMD runs (anelastic_sweep.lammps).

Amplitude rule (shear strain):  gamma0 = clip(gdot0 / w, gmin, gmax)              (Eq. 1)
  constant strain-rate amplitude gdot0 keeps the Weissenberg number gdot0*tau fixed,
  gmin keeps the stress signal above noise at high frequency, gmax caps large strains.

Run length from the fluctuation-dissipation estimate of the lock-in error on G'':
  dsigma^2 = C kT G''(w) / (w V T_run)                                            (Eq. 2)
  rel. error  e = dsigma / (G'' gamma0)  ->  T_run = C kT / (w G'' V gamma0^2 e^2)  (Eq. 3)
C = 8 was calibrated on the 8000 K, 1 THz, gamma0 = 0.0066 shear run (54671682).
G''(w) is guessed from a Maxwell model  G* = Ginf i w tau / (1 + i w tau)         (Eq. 4)
T_run is then raised to at least ncyc_min periods and rounded up to whole chunks.

Writes ROOT/tasks.txt (one line per array task) and ROOT/sweep.sh (SLURM array job,
header copied from the user's P100 submit script) and a task directory per run.
"""
13:T_run is then raised to at least ncyc_min periods and rounded up to whole chunks.
33:    p.add_argument('--periods', default='0.2,1,5,20,100',
34:                   help='drive periods in ps (each *1000 must be an integer)')
```

```
#!/bin/bash
source /resnick/home/recohen/.bashrc >/dev/null 2>&1
shopt -s expand_aliases
module load cuda >/dev/null 2>&1
conda activate deepmd_py311
export LAMMPS_PLUGIN_PATH=$CONDA_PREFIX/lib:$LAMMPS_PLUGIN_PATH OMP_NUM_THREADS=4 DP_INTER_OP_PARALLELISM_THREADS=2 DP_INTRA_OP_PARALLELISM_THREADS=2
ROOT=$(dirname $(readlink -f $0)); cd $ROOT/P0.2ps_r0
$CONDA_PREFIX/bin/lmp -in $ROOT/anelastic_sweep.lammps -var MODE uniaxial -var TEMP 8000 -var PERIOD 0.2 -var AMP 0.904303 \
  -var NTOT 400 -var NCHUNK 1 -var NAV 1 -var SEED 491001 -var NEQ 100 -var MAXCHUNKS 2 -var MODEL $ROOT/graph_c.pb \
  -var DATA $ROOT/MgO4096_Liq12000.in.pos -var TIMEOUT 3000 -log log.test > screen.test 2>&1
EOF
chmod +x $D/test.sh; squeue -j 4099863 -h -o "%T %L"; timeout 600 srun --jobid=4099863 --overlap -n1 -c4 $D/test.sh; echo rc=$?; sleep 10; grep -E "ERROR|deform|Loop time" $T/log.test | head; ls $T; head -3 $T/strain.dat; awk '!/^#/{print $3}' $T/strain.dat | sort -g | sed -n '1p;$p'
```

```
f='anelastic_sweep.lammps'; s=open(f).read()
def rep(o,n):
    global s; assert o in s, o; s=s.replace(o,n)
rep('if "$(is_file(restart.mgo))" then "read_restart restart.mgo" else "read_data ${DATA}"',
'''# restart.mgo: continue production; eq.data: equilibrated, production not started; else start
variable        HAVE_RS equal is_file(restart.mgo)
variable        HAVE_EQ equal is_file(eq.data)
if "${HAVE_RS}" then "read_restart restart.mgo" elif "${HAVE_EQ}" then "read_data eq.data" else "read_data ${DATA}"''')
rep('thermo_style    custom step temp press pxz xz cpuremain','thermo_style    custom step temp press pe pxz xz cpuremain   # pe needed by v_pe')
rep('if "$(is_file(restart.mgo)) == 0" then &','if "${HAVE_RS} == 0 && ${HAVE_EQ} == 0" then &')
open(f,'w').write(s)
EOF
```

### Claude (10-06 19:02)

The shear sweep (array 4113924) is now in production on all five tasks. Getting there took two failed submissions:
- **Input bug, `pe`:** the input averaged a potential-energy variable that `thermo_style` didn't include, so all five tasks died at the start of production. I added `pe` to `thermo_style`.
- **Input bug, `elif`:** my first restart fix had an `elif` syntax error. My single test hadn't exercised that branch.

Equilibration ran only once: the input now starts production from the saved `eq.data` when no restart exists. I then tested all three start paths on your GPU node: fresh, from `eq.data`, and from `restart.mgo`. The box tilt is oscillating, and speed is about 22.7 steps/s. The 0.2 ps point should finish in about 15 minutes and I'll run the lock-in then. The 100 ps point should take about 4 h.

**x uniaxial sweep:** it's set up in `/groups/carnegie_poc/rcohen/LAMMPS/MgO/4096atoms/V6.6868/4096atom/8000K/FreqSweep_x` and **not submitted**.
- **Method:** `fix deform x wiggle` with `nvt/sllod`, as in your R1A1 run, at a fixed strain amplitude ε₀ = 0.03 (0.90 Å on 30.14 Å).
- **Tested:** a short run on your GPU node gave the right L_x oscillation, and the lock-in tool now reads σ_xx and ε = ln(L_x/L̄_x) from the sweep directories.
- **Run lengths** come from a guessed Maxwell model for the longitudinal loss M″, scaled to your R1A1 result (M″ = 89 GPa at 1 THz).

**The catch is the low-frequency end.** The longitudinal loss M″ falls in proportion to frequency, while the noise in σ_xx does not. Cost:

| Period (ps) | Run | Expected error on M″ | Wall time at ~22 steps/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2, 1, 5 | 20 ps each | 1–9% | about 30 min each |
| 20 | 0.3 ns | 10% | about 4 h |
| 100 | 1.1 ns (capped) | 25% | about 14 h |

That totals 1.6 M steps, about 0.8 P100-days. Getting the 100 ps point to 10% would take 7.1 ns, about 3.7 P100-days. M′ will be accurate at every period; only the loss part is costly.

Should I submit it as above, drop the 100 ps point, or use a larger strain? ε₀ = 0.05 would cut the run times by 2.8×, at more risk of nonlinear response.

All of this is in section 0i of the restart notes.

### Claude (10-06 19:21)

I submitted the uniaxial x sweep as array 4115169, and the first shear point (0.2 ps) has finished. The uniaxial tasks were still waiting in the queue on priority; I'll check that they reach production without errors.

**Lock-in, xz shear, period 0.2 ps (5 THz):** 98 cycles at strain amplitude γ₀ = 0.010.

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| G′ | 87.2 ± 5.6 GPa |
| G″ | 98.0 ± 5.6 GPa |
| Phase δ | 48.4° (tan δ = 1.13) |
| η′ = G″/ω | 3.12 ± 0.18 mPa·s |
| Harmonics \|σ₂/σ₁\|, \|σ₃/σ₁\| | 0.010, 0.057 |

- **Error bar:** about 6%, a little better than the 9% the generator predicted.
- **Harmonics:** the third-harmonic ratio of 5.7% is the same size as the error on the fundamental, so it's noise, not a nonlinear response.
- **Against the 1 THz run (54671682, G′ = 15.5 ± 3.6, G″ = 42.9 ± 4.9 GPa, η′ = 6.8 mPa·s):** at 5 THz the response is more solid-like, and η′ has fallen to about half.
- **Against a single relaxation time:** the guess I used for run lengths (G∞ = 135 GPa, τ = 0.057 ps) predicts G′ ≈ 103 and G″ ≈ 58 GPa at 5 THz. The measured G″ is well above that, so the relaxation spreads over a range of times rather than one. That's the relaxation spectrum we're after.

The other shear points should finish over the next few hours, the 100 ps point last at around 23:00. The result is in the restart notes.

### Claude (10-06 19:24)

The linearity test at 20 ps would take about **6 hours** on one P100 (0.25 P100-days). It fits in one 24 h job and would finish well before the uniaxial 100 ps point (about 14 h).

| | Main 20 ps point (running) | Linearity repeat |
|---|---|---|
| Strain-rate amplitude γ̇₀ | 10¹¹ s⁻¹ | 3×10¹⁰ s⁻¹ |
| Strain amplitude γ₀ | 0.32 | 0.095 |
| Production | 60 ps (3 cycles) | 480 ps (24 cycles) |
| Steps | 60 k | 480 k |
| Wall time at 22.7 steps/s | ~45 min | ~5.9 h |
| Expected error on G″ | 8% | 10% |

- **Why it's 8× longer:** the stress signal falls with the strain amplitude, about 3.4× lower here. The noise doesn't, so it needs about 8× more cycles to reach a similar error.
- **Equilibration:** it can start from the existing `eq.data` of the 20 ps shear task and skip it, saving about 15 minutes. That shares the starting configuration but not the trajectory, so the two points are still independent measurements.

I can submit it now as a one-task array in `FreqSweep_lin20` and add it to the watcher. Want me to?

### Claude (10-06 19:43)

Two more shear points are done, so three of the five shear periods are now in:

| Period | f | G′ (GPa) | G″ (GPa) | δ | η′ = G″/ω (mPa·s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2 ps | 5 THz | 87.2 ± 5.6 | 98.0 ± 5.6 | 48.4° | 3.12 ± 0.18 |
| 1 ps | 1 THz | 16.7 ± 2.7 | 44.8 ± 1.1 | 69.6° | 7.14 ± 0.17 |
| 5 ps | 200 GHz | 1.6 ± 0.9 | 9.96 ± 0.17 | 81.0° | 7.92 ± 0.13 |

- **The 1 THz point reproduces your earlier run 54671682** (G′ = 15.5 ± 3.6, G″ = 42.9 ± 4.9 GPa) with error bars 2–4× smaller. That validates the new input and analysis on real MgO.
- **The viscosity is levelling off.** η′ rises 3.1 → 7.1 → 7.9 mPa·s as frequency drops, approaching the low-frequency limit η₀. The single-relaxation-time estimate was η₀ ≈ 7.7 mPa·s, so η₀ is about 8 mPa·s at 8000 K. At 200 GHz the liquid is almost purely viscous: δ = 81°, and G′ is barely above zero.
- **Harmonics:** at 5 ps the third harmonic is 9% of the fundamental. That's about the size of the noise on the in-phase stress, so probably not real nonlinearity. The 20 ps linearity test will settle it.

The 20 ps shear point should finish within the hour, the 100 ps shear point around 23:00, and the linearity test around 01:45. Results are added to the restart file as they come in.

### Claude (10-06 19:46)

I found a probable problem with the **uniaxial** sweep, and a cheap test to settle it. The shear results are not affected.

**What the data show.** Lock-in of the temperature against the strain:

| Run | T oscillation at drive frequency | at 2× frequency |
|---|---|---|
| uniaxial, 0.2 ps | 432 K | 1024 K |
| uniaxial, 1 ps | 294 K | 1 K |
| uniaxial, 5 ps | 6 K | 1 K |
| shear, 0.2 ps | 6 K | 119 K |
| shear, 1 ps | 4 K | 20 K |

- **Uniaxial:** compressing and expanding the liquid heats and cools it. The ratio corresponds to a Grüneisen parameter of about 1.2–1.8. The thermostat (Nosé–Hoover, τ_T = 0.1 ps) then pulls that heat in and out with a lag.
- **Why that matters:** the thermostat's lag adds a loss to M″ that isn't in the liquid. It peaks near f ≈ 1/(2π τ_T) ≈ 1.6 THz, right where we measure.
- **Size:** I estimate ΔM ≈ ρ C_v γ² T ≈ 75–160 GPa, which gives up to about 40–80 GPa of artificial M″ at the peak. Your R1A1 result is M″ = 89 GPa at 1 THz with the same thermostat. So part of the existing ζ + (4/3)η at 1 THz may be this artifact, not bulk viscosity.
- **Shear:** it barely changes the volume, so the in-phase temperature swing is only 4–6 K. Its 2× frequency signal is ordinary viscous heating, and the thermostat doesn't distort G″.

I'm confident about the size of the effect. I'm less sure of the details: the phase of the temperature at 1 ps doesn't fit a simple model.

**Proposed test (about 40 min on two P100s):** rerun the uniaxial 1 ps point from its `eq.data` with τ_T = 1 ps and 0.02 ps; the current run is 0.1 ps. If M″ changes with τ_T, the thermostat is contaminating it, and the uniaxial runs need a different thermostat setting for each frequency. One catch: at 5 THz and ε₀ = 0.03, viscous heating is about 340 K/ps, so a weak thermostat there needs a smaller strain amplitude too.

The uniaxial sweep keeps running in the meantime, so we lose nothing. Should I run the test?

### Claude (10-06 19:50)

Two uniaxial points are in, and they raise a flag:

| Period | f | M′ (GPa) | M″ (GPa) | δ | M″/ω = ζ + (4/3)η (mPa·s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.2 ps | 5 THz | 769.4 ± 2.6 | 109.6 ± 2.0 | 8.1° | 3.49 ± 0.06 |
| (R1A1, your run, ε₀ = 0.033) | 1 THz | 537.5 ± 1.6 | 89.1 ± 0.9 | 9.4° | 14.2 ± 0.1 |
| 5 ps | 200 GHz | 579.6 ± 1.9 | 13.5 ± 10.5 | 1.3° | 11 ± 8 |

- **M′ goes up and down with frequency:** 769 → 537 → 580 GPa. A single relaxing material should stiffen steadily with frequency. But the 1 THz value comes from your separate R1A1 run, so I'll compare against the sweep's own 1 THz point, due shortly, before reading anything into it.
- **Some of the 5 THz stiffening is shear:** from 1 to 5 THz, M′ rises by 232 GPa. The shear part, (4/3)ΔG′, accounts for about 94 GPa. The remaining ~138 GPa is in the bulk part, which is the size I estimated for the adiabatic–isothermal (thermostat) effect. That's consistent with the concern I raised, though not proof.
- **The 5 ps M″ error is 77%, not the predicted 9%.** Either the noise constant I assumed for uniaxial runs (C = 1, taken from your R1A1 error bar) is too optimistic, or 3.7 cycles is too few. M′ is well determined at every point.
- **The 5 THz point has a large second harmonic** (|σ₂/σ₁| = 0.15) at ε₀ = 0.03. That's a nonlinear elastic response, probably from the pressure dependence of K. A smaller strain would reduce it.

The thermostat test I proposed (rerun the 1 ps uniaxial point with τ_T = 1 ps and 0.02 ps) would separate these effects. It's still waiting on your go-ahead.

### Claude (10-06 20:02)

The sweep's own 1 THz uniaxial point reproduces your R1A1 run. So the non-monotonic M′ is real in the simulation, not a difference between runs:

| f | M′ (GPa) | M″ (GPa) | T oscillation at drive frequency (amplitude, phase vs strain) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 THz | 769.4 ± 2.6 | 109.6 ± 2.0 | 432 K, −175° |
| 1 THz | **540.5 ± 3.8** | **92.7 ± 3.9** | **294 K, −15°** |
| (R1A1) 1 THz | 537.5 ± 1.6 | 89.1 ± 0.9 | |
| 200 GHz | 579.6 ± 1.9 | 13.5 ± 10.5 | 6 K, −4° |

This now points more specifically at the **Nosé–Hoover thermostat resonating**, not just relaxing:
- **Phase:** at 5 THz, T swings opposite to the strain, which is plain adiabatic cooling on expansion. At 1 THz the swing is still large (294 K) but almost in phase with the strain. A large amplitude with a flipped phase is what a driven oscillator does near its natural frequency.
- **Natural frequency:** Nosé–Hoover is a second-order (oscillator-like) coupling. With T_damp = 0.1 ps its natural frequency is ~1.6–10 THz, so 1 THz is close.
- **M′:** a simple relaxing material can't show a dip in M′ like this; a resonance can.
- **Consequence:** the uniaxial M′ and M″ near 1 THz, including R1A1's ζ + (4/3)η = 14 mPa·s, are probably distorted by the thermostat.
- **Shear is unaffected:** its temperature swing at the drive frequency is only 4–6 K.

**A fix that saves the runs already done.** The extra stress comes from the temperature swing: δσ_xx = −(∂P/∂T)_V δT(t). We record T every step, so the lock-in can fit the stress to strain and temperature together. That removes the thermostat's contribution and leaves the isothermal complex modulus at every frequency. The adiabatic modulus is that plus a known constant.

This needs one number, (∂P/∂T)_V. Two 20 ps NVT runs at 7800 and 8200 K from `eq.data` give it, about 15 min each on a P100.

**Proposal, about 1 GPU-hour in total:**
1. Two NVT runs for (∂P/∂T)_V.

Rather than restarting the 30-minute watcher repeatedly, I'll give the watcher a one-shot mode: it exits after the first new result, which notifies me once, and I restart it then.

**Tool `Bash`** — Add one-shot mode to watcher

```
cd /resnick/home/recohen/Claude/tools; python3 - <<'EOF'
f='sweep_watch.sh'; s=open(f).read()
s=s.replace("# usage: sweep_watch.sh ROOT:JOBID:MODE [ROOT:JOBID:MODE ...]","# usage: sweep_watch.sh ROOT:JOBID:MODE [ROOT:JOBID:MODE ...]\n# ONESHOT=1: exit after the first loop pass that reports something (for background use).")
s=s.replace("while true; do\n  active=0","while true; do\n  active=0; reported=0")
for tag in ['echo "DONE $D','echo "FAILED $D','echo "INCOMPLETE $D']:
    i=s.index(tag); j=s.index('\n',i)
    s=s[:j]+'; reported=1'+s[j:]
s=s.replace('  [ $active -eq 0 ] && { echo "ALL DONE"; exit 0; }','  [ $active -eq 0 ] && { echo "ALL DONE"; exit 0; }\n  [ -n "$ONESHOT" ] && [ $reported -eq 1 ] && exit 0')
open(f,'w').write(s)
EOF
bash -n sweep_watch.sh && grep -nE "reported|ONESHOT" sweep_watch.sh
```

### Claude (10-06 22:43)

All five shear points are done. The last one (P = 100 ps, 10 GHz, γ₀ = 1.59) finished at 22:43:

- G″ = 0.503 ± 0.027 GPa, so η′ = 8.00 ± 0.42 mPa·s.
- G′ = −0.024 ± 0.003 GPa. A negative value is unphysical, so read it as G′ ≈ 0. The run covers only 2.7 cycles analysed in 3 blocks, so that error bar is probably too small.
- The harmonics are small (|σ₂/σ₁| = 0.08, |σ₃/σ₁| = 0.03), so the response is still linear even at γ₀ = 1.59.

**Shear sweep at 8000 K:**

| f | G′ (GPa) | G″ (GPa) | η′ (mPa·s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 THz | 87 ± 6 | 98 ± 6 | 3.1 |
| 1 THz | 17 ± 3 | 45 ± 1 | 7.1 |
| 200 GHz | 1.6 ± 0.9 | 10.0 ± 0.2 | 7.9 |
| 50 GHz | 0.1 ± 0.5 | 2.4 ± 0.3 | 7.6 |
| 10 GHz | ≈ 0 | 0.50 ± 0.03 | 8.0 |

η′ levels off at η₀ ≈ 8 mPa·s for frequencies of 200 GHz and below, and the viscoelastic crossover sits between 1 and 5 THz.

I've added this to `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-06.md` and restarted the watcher on the two runs still going.

**Still running:**
- **Uniaxial P = 20 ps:** about 3.4 h in, of roughly 4 h, so due shortly.
- **Uniaxial P = 100 ps:** about 14 h in total.
- **20 ps linearity test:** due around 01:45.

The temperature-correction runs for the uniaxial points are still waiting on your go-ahead.

### Claude (10-06 23:09)

The 20 ps uniaxial point (50 GHz, 13 cycles) finished at 23:05:

- M′ = 582.0 ± 1.1 GPa and M″ = 3.9 ± 1.0 GPa (δ = 0.4°).
- M″/ω = ζ + 4/3 η = 12.5 ± 3.0 mPa·s. Taking η₀ ≈ 8 mPa·s from the shear sweep leaves a bulk viscosity ζ ≈ 1.8 ± 3 mPa·s, which is not distinguishable from zero.
- The temperature swing at the drive frequency is only 0.4 K, so the thermostat does not affect this point.

**This supports the thermostat explanation for the 1 THz uniaxial value:**

| f | M′ (GPa) |
|---|---|
| 5 THz | 769 |
| 1 THz | 540 (this sweep and the older R1A1 run agree) |
| 200 GHz | 580 |
| 50 GHz | 582 |

The 200 and 50 GHz values agree, so the fully relaxed modulus is about 581 GPa. For a passive viscoelastic liquid, M′ can only rise with frequency, so it can never drop below the relaxed value. The 540 GPa at 1 THz does, where the temperature swing was 294 K. That makes the 1 THz uniaxial number an artefact, very likely from the Nosé–Hoover thermostat. The proposed thermostat runs (τ_T = 1 and 0.02 ps) would confirm or rule this out; they still need your go-ahead.

I've added this to the restart doc and restarted the watcher. Still running: uniaxial P = 100 ps (about 10 h left) and the 20 ps linearity test (around 01:45).

### Claude (10-07 01:05)

The linearity test finished at about 01:00, and it shows the 50 GHz shear point is in the linear regime.

| run | γ₀ | cycles | G″ (GPa) | η′ (mPa·s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| main sweep, P = 20 ps | 0.32 | 2.7 | 2.38 ± 0.29 | 7.6 ± 0.9 |
| linearity test | 0.095 | 22 | 2.57 ± 0.11 | 8.19 ± 0.36 |

- **Amplitude effect:** the two η′ values differ by 0.6 ± 1.0 mPa·s, so there is no measurable dependence on strain amplitude.
- **Best 50 GHz value:** the weighted mean is η′ = 8.13 ± 0.34 mPa·s.
- **Harmonics:** |σ₃/σ₁| is 0.097 at the smaller amplitude, against 0.17 at the larger one. Both are at the noise level, so the earlier 0.17 was noise, not real nonlinearity.

With the plateau at 200, 50 and 10 GHz, η₀ ≈ 8 mPa·s for MgO liquid at 8000 K.

The restart doc is updated. Only the 100 ps uniaxial run is left, about 6 h into its roughly 14 h, so it should finish around 09:00. The watcher is running on it.

### Claude (10-07 08:36)

Both 8000 K sweeps are finished. The last point, uniaxial at 100 ps (10 GHz), ran 9 cycles in 13.2 h.

| f | M′ (GPa) | M″ (GPa) | M″/ω (mPa·s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 THz | 769.4 ± 2.6 | 109.6 ± 2.0 | 3.5 |
| 1 THz | 540.5 ± 3.8 | 92.7 ± 3.9 | 14.8 (thermostat artefact suspected) |
| 200 GHz | 579.6 ± 1.9 | 13.5 ± 10.5 | 11 ± 8 |
| 50 GHz | 582.0 ± 1.1 | 3.9 ± 1.0 | 12.5 ± 3.0 |
| 10 GHz | 581.9 ± 1.0 | 1.16 ± 0.64 | 18 ± 10 |

- **Relaxed bulk modulus:** K₀ = 581.9 ± 0.7 GPa. The 200, 50 and 10 GHz points agree.
- **Longitudinal viscosity:** combining the 50 and 10 GHz points gives ζ + 4/3 η = 13.0 ± 2.9 mPa·s. With η₀ ≈ 8 mPa·s from the shear sweep, the bulk viscosity is ζ ≈ 2 ± 3 mPa·s, so it is small and only an upper bound at this precision.
- **Error estimate was too optimistic:** the 10 GHz M″ error came out at 55 %, against 25 % predicted. Pinning ζ down further would need runs 2–5 times longer than the noise model said.
- **1 THz point:** it still looks like a thermostat artefact, since its M′ falls below the relaxed value. The τ_T test runs to check this are still waiting on your go-ahead.

I've updated `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-06.md` and added a 2026-10-07 entry to `ANELASTICITY_DONE.md`. The watcher has exited because nothing from these sweeps is left in the queue.

Your queue now has other sweeps running at 6000, 7000 and 8000 K (MgOsh6k/7k, MgOux6k/7k/8k) plus Green–Kubo jobs (GK4000–8000). I haven't touched them. If you want lock-in analysis on those as they finish, tell me which sweep directories and job IDs and I'll set up the watcher the same way.

```
Read with `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ANELASTICITY_TODO.md`, `ANELASTICITY_DONE.md`, `ANELASTICITY_PLAN.md`. `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-06.md` still holds the GPU fix (§1), sweep design (§2), the full result tables (§3), and the tools (§5); this file adds what changed on 10-07. ## 0. What to check FIRST

3. Analyse: `python tools/gk_viscosity.py <dir>/pres.dat --tmax 5 --nblock 8` (cohen_env). Writes `gk_G.dat`, `gk_spectrum.dat` next to `pres.dat`. Check the NVE drift line and that `msd.dat` grows linearly at 7000 K (liquid). The MSD reference resets at each restart.

## 1. Green–Kubo runs

`tools/gk_stress.lammps`: reads `DATA`, rescales velocities to TEMP, NVT (Nosé–Hoover, 0.1 ps) for NEQ steps, writes `eq.data`, then **NVE** production with the pressure tensor every 2 fs (`pres.dat`: step pxx pyy pzz pxy pxz pyz temp pe etotal, bar). NVE keeps the thermostat out of the stress correlations, which matters for the bulk channel (§3).
```

### Claude (10-07 09:10)

You're right that a Claude session started them, but it wasn't this one. Another session in this project submitted every job from 4145234 onward overnight and recorded them in `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-07.md`. This session had no record of that, which is why I misreported them as unknown.

**That session already ran the thermostat test, so my earlier conclusions were wrong:**
- It ran the τ_T test (job 4145234) at about 03:30. I kept saying that test was waiting on your go-ahead, which was not true.
- It found that with τ_T = 0.1 ps the thermostat adds loss to the uniaxial M″ at every frequency, not just 1 THz. So the ζ ≈ 2 ± 3 mPa·s I reported from this sweep can't be used.
- The low-frequency M′ ≈ 582 GPa is the isothermal bulk modulus K_T, not the relaxed modulus in general.
- The uniaxial sweep is being rerun with the new thermostat settings (4147331, `FreqSweep_x2`).

**Docs corrected:**
- `ANELASTICITY_RESTART_2026-10-06.md` and `ANELASTICITY_DONE.md` now point to the 10-07 findings, so my 08:36 entries no longer give the bad ζ.
- I added the P100 result to the 10-07 restart (§8).
- I removed the finished item T4b from `ANELASTICITY_TODO.md`.

**What's running now** (all from that session):
- **Uniaxial sweeps:** 8000 K rerun (4147331), 7000 K (4147085) and 6000 K (4151696).
- **Shear sweeps:** 7000 K (4147084) and 6000 K (4151695).
- **Green–Kubo runs:** 4000–8000 K.
- **5000 K:** it crystallized, so no sweep has been set up there yet. Its sweeps need a decision from you on the solid-state protocol (§7 of the 10-07 restart).

That session is still adding lock-in results for every finished task, so I haven't started a second watcher. Two watchers writing to the same `lockin_results.txt` files could produce duplicate lines. If that session has ended, tell me and I'll take over the watching and the final run of `anelastic_collect.py`.
